Controlled launch and termination

  • Lifecycle hook: Pauses ASG instance launch or termination so required work can finish.
  • Launch example: Install and check the application before the instance starts taking work.
  • Termination example: Finish ongoing work or connections before the instance shuts down.
  • Mixed Instances Policy: Lets an ASG combine instance types and On-Demand/Spot to improve capacity availability and cost.

4. Practical example

A website requires at least two EC2 instances; its ASG replaces an unhealthy instance.

Web fleet multiple AZs after ALB; SQS processing worker can create/delete according to backlog; Patch rollout using new AMI and Instance Refresh.

5. Comparison

A launch template describes instances; the ASG manages the fleet; scaling policies change desired capacity.

ASG scaling policies, metrics & cooldowns

1. Concept

Scaling policies add or remove EC2 instances based on usage metrics or schedules.

2. Purpose

Increase capacity when lacking capacity and reduce capacity when excess, based on signals reflecting application load.

3. How it works

Stream scales according to load:

Metric / Alarm
↓ Scaling Policy
Auto Scaling Group
↓ increase or decrease desired capacity
EC2 instances

Scaling types

PolicyHow it worksFor example
Target trackingKeep metrics close to targetAverage CPU is about 50%
Step scalingThe more you exceed the threshold, the stronger the increaseCPU 70–85% add 1; above 85% add 3
Simple scalingAn alarm triggers a change, then waits for a cooldownHigh CPU → add 2 machines
ScheduledChange capacity according to known scheduleAdd machines before peak hours
PredictiveForecast from historical patterns to prepare in advanceThe load increases repeatedly every morning

Select metrics

MetricSuitable workload
CPUA CPU-bound application.
ALBRequestCountPerTargetA web application with reasonably similar requests.
Network metricA bandwidth-bound workload.
SQS backlog per instanceQueue workers; measure pending messages per worker rather than only total messages.
  • SQS example: 1,000 messages with 2 workers impose a different load from 1,000 messages with 100 workers.
  • Target tracking: the metric must reflect usage per unit of capacity.

Warmup, grace period and cooldown

ConceptPurpose
WarmupLets a new instance stabilize before being appropriately included in scaling decisions.
Health check grace periodPrevents an instance from being judged unhealthy too soon after launch.
CooldownMainly used for simple scaling to avoid repeated reactions before earlier scaling takes effect.
  • Target tracking and step scaling use warmup.
  • There is no universal rule that all scaling is locked for 300 seconds.

Increase quickly, decrease cautiously

  • Fast startup: Prepare an AMI with software installed so new instances become ready sooner.
  • Scaling in: Allow requests and jobs to finish before removing instances; use deregistration delay or lifecycle hooks where needed.
  • Scale-out limit: Set a sufficient maximum and monitor quotas and AWS capacity availability.

4. Practical example

Order-processing workers scale out when the queued messages per worker exceed their target.

Web volatility: target tracking. Campaign knows start time: scheduled. SQS backlog grows very fast: step scaling or target tracking on backlog per worker.

5. Comparison

Target tracking maintains a metric target; step scaling responds to threshold ranges; scheduled scaling follows a timetable.

AWS Elastic Beanstalk

1. Concept

Elastic Beanstalk deploys an application to AWS and manages the infrastructure needed to run it.

2. Purpose

Elastic Beanstalk helps deploy web applications to AWS quickly without having to manually build each part such as EC2, Auto Scaling, Load Balancer and monitoring.

3. How it works

Code
↓
Elastic Beanstalk
↓
EC2 + ASG + Load Balancer

Elastic Beanstalk = PaaS, not serverless.

How it works

  • Developers mainly worry about: Application Code
  • Beanstalk supports: Provision infrastructure · Deploy application · Auto Scaling · Load Balancing · Health Monitoring
  • Support: Java · .NET · Node.js · Python · Go · PHP · Docker
  • You can still access and configure the resources below.

Web Server Tier

  • Used for web/API to receive HTTP requests.
Client
↓
Load Balancer
↓
EC2 in Auto Scaling Group
  • Suitable: Website · REST API · Synchronous Request

Worker Tier

  • Used for background jobs.
Web App
↓
SQS
↓
Worker Environment
↓
Process Job

For example:

  • Send Email · Resize Image · Generate Report

Web Tier = HTTP Worker Tier = SQS + async job

Deployment

  • Important deployment types: Rolling = update each group of instances
  • Immutable = create a new instance and replace the old instance
  • Traffic Splitting = divide traffic between old and new versions

Database

Production database should be separate from the Beanstalk lifecycle:

Elastic Beanstalk
↓
Application
↓
Private RDS
  • → Terminating the environment does not destroy the production database.

Cost

  • Elastic Beanstalk itself has no separate fees.
  • You pay for the resources below: EC2 · Load Balancer · RDS · S3 · ...

AWS App Runner

  • AWS App Runner Run web applications or APIs from source code or container images without needing to manage EC2, ECS or Kubernetes yourself. Services take care of infrastructure, deployment, load balancing, HTTPS endpoint and automatic scaling.
Java Spring Boot REST API
↓
Source code / Container image
↓
AWS App Runner
↓ self-deploy and self-scale
HTTPS Endpoint
↓
Users calls GET /products
  • For example, package the Spring Boot backend into a container image on ECR, deploy with App Runner for users to call GET /products. App Runner matches the receiving application HTTP requests; Scheduled batch jobs should use a dispatch service like AWS Batch combined with EventBridge Scheduler.
  • Compared to Beanstalk, App Runner reduces web/container infrastructure management;
  • Beanstalk allows accessing and customizing the underlying EC2/ASG/Load Balancer resources.
  • Updated from April 30, 2026: App Runner is not accepting new customers; Existing customers continue to use it. AWS recommends ECS Express Mode for new needs; still keep the App Runner concept to learn SAA-C03 material.

4. Practical example

A team uploads its web application; Beanstalk creates the configured EC2, load-balancing, and Auto Scaling environment.

  • Team wants to deploy web app quickly but still uses EC2/ASG.
  • The Web tier writes the job to SQS and the Worker tier processes it.
  • Traditional applications need a managed platform rather than building the infrastructure yourself.

5. Comparison

Beanstalk manages application environments; App Runner simplifies running web/container applications; ECS manages container orchestration.

Compare App Runner vs Elastic Beanstalk vs ECS Express Mode & Deployment Type

ServiceInputInfrastructure management level
AWS App RunnerSource code or Container imageFully managed (HTTPS, scaling, automatic LB); Minimum configuration required
AWS Elastic BeanstalkApplication code / packageAWS builds EC2 + ALB + ASG but you still retain SSH/customization of the underlying EC2
ECS Express ModeContainer image + IAM RolesQuickly create ECS Service yourself on Fargate + ALB + Auto Scaling + HTTPS in your account
Rolling Deployment → Gradually replace instances/tasks on the existing environment
Blue/Green Deployment → Create a new environment in parallel and then transfer traffic (instant rollback)

Summary

  • Load Balancing & Availability: A load balancer sends traffic to healthy instances. More instances share demand; multiple AZs improve fault tolerance.
  • ALB, NLB & GWLB: ALB routes HTTP web traffic. NLB handles TCP/UDP connections. GWLB sends traffic through security inspection appliances.
  • Health Checks, Sessions & TLS: Health checks test backends. Sticky sessions keep a client on the same backend. TLS encrypts connections; deregistration delay lets existing requests finish.
  • Auto Scaling Groups: An ASG maintains the required EC2 fleet and replaces failed instances. A launch template supplies the configuration for new instances.
  • Scaling Policies: Target tracking adjusts capacity toward a metric target. Step scaling responds to load thresholds. Scheduled scaling changes capacity on a timetable.
  • Elastic Beanstalk: You upload an application; Beanstalk sets up and manages its infrastructure. Web tiers accept HTTP, while worker tiers process SQS jobs.

RDS, Aurora & ElastiCache

Amazon RDS fundamentals & operations

1. Concept

RDS runs relational databases such as MySQL or PostgreSQL and manages servers, system updates, and configured backups.

2. Purpose

Run a relational database without having to manage the entire server, patches and backups yourself.

3. How it works

Database flow:

Application
↓ Database endpoint
RDS
↓
Managed database / storage / backup

What does RDS manage, what do you manage?

  • RDS supports engines such as MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, Oracle, SQL Server and Db2;
  • Aurora is a MySQL/PostgreSQL compatible option with its own architecture.
  • AWS takes care of provisioning, OS patching, and configured managed features.
  • You still design the schema, query, index, database users, capacity, backup retention and network security.

Configure database

  • Select engine/version, instance class, storage, subnet group, SG, parameter/option group and maintenance window.
  • Set DB in private subnets; Just put the SG app into the database port.
  • Changing instance class increases CPU/RAM; Add replica extension read.
  • Storage Auto Scaling increases disk within the set maximum storage, does not automatically increase CPU and does not automatically reduce disk.

Storage Auto Scaling

  • RDS increases storage when low free space is persistent and enough time has elapsed since the previous change.
  • Typical conditions: less than 10% left, lasts at least 5 minutes, has been at least 6 hours since the change or storage optimization has been completed, set a later date.
  • Set maximum large enough and alarm monitors storage.
  • Auto Scaling does not replace data cleaning or capacity planning.

RDS vs. EC2 and RDS Custom

  • RDS usually does not allow SSH into the OS.
  • DB on EC2 gives more control but you take care of patching, backup and HA yourself.
  • RDS Custom for Oracle/SQL Server needs deeper customization in OS/database.
  • Changes must still be within the scope of support and coordination with the service's automation.

CloudWatch and Enhanced Monitoring

RDS monitoring modeCollection Cycle & Mechanism
RDS CloudWatch Metrics (Default)Send metrics at each hypervisor/database layer 1 minute (no need to enable EC2 Detailed Monitoring)
Enhanced MonitoringThe agent right on the DB instance collects deep OS/process metrics with cycles down to 1 second and send it back CloudWatch Logs (need monitoring IAM role)
  • RDS CloudWatch metrics: Report database/hypervisor metrics every minute without EC2 Detailed Monitoring.
  • Enhanced Monitoring: An agent on the DB instance collects deeper OS and process metrics at intervals down to one second; a monitoring IAM role is required.
  • Viewing data: The console refreshes as often as every five seconds. Read CloudWatch Logs for the one-second collection data.

RDS Storage Types, Parameter Groups, Private Endpoint & RDS Custom

RDS StoragePerformance characteristics
gp2Previous General Purpose SSD; IOPS is tied to capacity + burst credits
gp3General Purpose SSD; Separately configure IOPS and throughput independently of capacity
io1 / io2 Block ExpressProvisioned IOPS SSD reserves stable, low-latency IOPS for heavy DB workloads
RDS componentsFunctions & Distinguishing Points
RDS Parameter GroupsWhere to configure engine parameters and DB security (eg limit connections, force SSL/TLS connections like rds.force_ssl)
RDS Private EndpointDNS hostname of DB resolves to Private IP in subnet when Public Access is turned off (not Interface VPC Endpoint)
RDS Custom for Oracle / SQL ServerManaged service but allows permissions privileged OS & DB access to install third-party software/plugins or use special BYOL

4. Practical example

A store keeps orders in RDS PostgreSQL instead of installing and patching PostgreSQL on EC2.

Web needs managed PostgreSQL/MySQL: RDS. Enterprise software needs OS customization: consider RDS Custom. Need HA: add Multi-AZ; need to reduce reading load: Read Replica; need to reduce repeated queries: cache.

5. Comparison

RDS manages many operational tasks; a database on EC2 offers deeper control with more operational responsibility.

RDS Multi-AZ & Read Replicas

1. Concept

Multi-AZ provides failover capacity when a database fails; read replicas take read queries off the primary database.

2. Purpose

Distinguish fault-tolerant solutions from solutions that increase read capacity.

3. How it works

Distinguish between two needs:

  • Multi-AZ = fault tolerance/failover
  • Read Replica = increased read load

Multi-AZ DB instance

  • Primary: The main database instance serving application queries.
  • Standby: An instance in another AZ receiving synchronous copies without serving reads.
  • Primary failure: RDS automatically fails over and updates the connection endpoint. Applications must reconnect, retry, and handle interrupted transactions.
  • Scope: Protects within one Region; does not replace backups for accidental deletion or cross-Region recovery.

Multi-AZ DB Cluster

  • Writer: One instance accepts writes.
  • Readers: Two instances in the other AZs can serve reads.
  • Replication: Semisynchronous: a write is acknowledged after at least one reader receives the change.
  • Writer failure: A reader can automatically take over as writer.
  • Compared with a DB instance: A Multi-AZ DB instance standby cannot serve reads; the cluster's two readers can.

Read Replica

  • Read Replica: A separate read-serving copy with its own application endpoint.
  • Freshness: Receives changes after the primary and can lag. Use the primary when the latest data is required.
  • Primary failure: An ordinary replica does not take over automatically. Promotion makes it independent and stops following its old source.
  • Location and count: Same- or cross-Region support, replica counts, and features depend on the engine.
  • Transfer cost: In-Region replication transfer is free where supported by RDS; cross-Region transfer is charged.

4. Practical example

An online store writes orders to the primary, sends reports to a Read Replica, and uses Multi-AZ for automatic failover.

DB production needs to failover itself when AZ fails: Multi-AZ. Heavy reports slow down transactions: Read Replica. Users in other Regions need to read more closely or have DR: cross-Region replica with promotion plan.

5. Comparison

  • A Multi-AZ DB instance has a non-readable standby;
  • Read Replicas serve reads;
  • a Multi-AZ DB cluster has two readers and automatic failover.

Key comparison

CriteriaMulti-AZ DB instanceRead Replica
TargetAvailability and automatic failoverRead scaling, reporting, can support DR
ReplicationSynchronous to standby in another AZAsynchronous, has replica lag
Standby serving reading?NoThere is an endpoint for the app to send read queries
When primary failsRDS moves the endpoint to standbyPromote according to the appropriate process, change the app endpoint

RTO, RPO, and failover

  • The RTO/RPO examples below compare recovery mechanisms rather than guarantee every failure outcome.
  • Engine, configuration, lag, and failure type matter; applications still need reconnect/retry logic and failover tests.
Deployment modelCommon RTORPOFailover Mechanism
RDS Read ReplicaSeveral minutes or moreDepends on replication lagManual promotion
RDS Multi-AZ DB Instance60–120 seconds0 (synchronous)Automatic
RDS Multi-AZ DB ClusterUnder 35 seconds0 (semi-sync quorum)Automatic
Aurora DB Cluster + ReplicaUsually less than 30 seconds0 (shared storage 6 copies/3 AZ)Automatic
Aurora Global Database (Cross-Region)Under 1 minute (managed)Usually < 1 second (async)The Managed/Manual Failover step needs to be enabled to promote the secondary cluster

Migration with less downtime: can create Aurora Read Replica directly from the running RDS MySQL/PostgreSQL, wait for replication to catch up and then promote to the independent Aurora cluster.

Amazon Aurora architecture & scaling

1. Concept

Aurora is MySQL/PostgreSQL-compatible; query-processing instances share a storage layer that maintains multiple data copies.

2. Purpose

Use a MySQL/PostgreSQL compatible database with distributed storage and integrated read scaling.

3. How it works

Two access routes:

Write → Writer endpoint → Primary instance
Read → Reader endpoint → Aurora Replicas
Compute → shared distributed storage

Compute is separate from storage

  • Database instance: The machine executing queries. Aurora typically has one writer and up to 15 readers (Aurora Replicas).
  • Storage: Instances share a storage layer whose capacity grows with the data.
  • Data protection: Storage keeps six copies across three AZs and repairs failed copies. These are data copies, not six database instances.
  • Quorum: The agreement mechanism is commonly described as four copies for writes and three for reads, rather than waiting for all six.

Writer and Reader endpoints

EndpointUse when
Writer/cluster endpointWrite or read-write; Points to the current writer
Reader endpointDistribute read connections across replicas
Instance endpointNeed a specific instance
Custom endpointSplit replica groups for a workload
  • The Reader endpoint is balanced at the connection level, not dividing each SQL query itself.
  • Without a replica, the reader endpoint can point to the writer; Do not consider the endpoint name as a read-only enforcement mechanism.

Failover

  • When the writer fails, Aurora can promote the replica and update the writer endpoint.
  • Placing the replica in another AZ helps restore compute faster; Multi-AZ storage by itself does not guarantee uninterrupted connectivity.
  • App still needs reconnect/retry.
  • Actual failover time depends on the situation; don't consider the quick numbers in the slide as an SLA for every defect.

Auto Scaling and custom endpoints

  • Aurora Auto Scaling: Adds or removes readers based on metrics such as CPU or connections to handle more reads.
  • Serverless v2: Changes the processing resources of an instance, rather than the number of readers.
  • Custom endpoint: Connects to a selected group of instances, such as stronger readers reserved for heavy reports.
  • Reader endpoint: Remains available for distributing read connections; the application chooses the endpoint appropriate for the work.

4. Practical example

A SaaS application writes transactions through the writer endpoint and runs reports on Aurora readers.

OLTP requires MySQL/PostgreSQL-compatible and HA; Read-intensive SaaS uses reader endpoint; Separate reporting from transaction query using replica/custom endpoint.

5. Comparison

The reader endpoint distributes read connections; the writer endpoint follows the current writer; an instance endpoint targets one instance.

Aurora Serverless, Global & advanced capabilities

1. Concept

Aurora can adjust processing capacity, maintain data in another Region, and create separate copies for testing.

2. Purpose

Handle demand spikes, serve distant users, and test changes without editing live data.

3. How it works

Aurora Serverless v2

  • How it works: adjusts processing power within your limits. You still configure the engine, network, and security.
  • Example: a shop gains processing power during a sale and reduces it afterward.
  • Difference: changes each instance's power rather than adding read-only instances.
  • Pausing when idle: requires a supported version and configuration.

Aurora Global Database

  • How it works: writes in one AWS Region are copied elsewhere for nearby reads.
  • Example: an order may be lost if the primary fails before the backup location receives it.
  • Recovery: switch the backup location to accept writes. Test possible data loss and recovery time.
  • Multi-AZ: provides in-Region redundancy, not a substitute for cross-Region recovery.

Database Cloning

  • Purpose: create a test copy; editing it does not change the original.
  • Fast creation: initially shares data and stores changed portions separately.
  • Example: test a table change or run a heavy report on the copy.
  • Remember: has separate compute and costs; does not replace recovery backups.

Babelfish and Machine Learning

  • Babelfish: helps SQL Server applications move to Aurora PostgreSQL with fewer code changes.
  • Example: reuse many existing sales-app statements; test because compatibility is not complete.
  • Machine learning: Aurora calls AI services such as SageMaker or Comprehend; requires supported versions and configuration.
  • Example: ask AI whether comments are positive or negative. The database sends data and receives results; it does not teach itself.

4. Practical example

Example: A shop uses Serverless v2 to increase database processing power during a sale. Before changing its orders table, the team creates a separate test copy with cloning.

5. Comparison

  • Serverless v2: increases or decreases each instance's processing power.
  • Reader Auto Scaling: adds or removes instances to share reads.
  • Global Database: keeps data in other Regions for distant users and disaster recovery.

Aurora Standard vs Aurora I/O-Optimized

Configure Aurora StorageHow to calculate feesWhen is it optimal?
Aurora StandardCharge for GB-month capacity + charge per read/write I/O operationWorkload has a moderate amount of I/O
Aurora I/O-OptimizedCompute & storage prices are higher but Completely free for each I/O requestI/O overhead exceeds approx 25% of total Aurora bill or need to forecast stable costs for large I/O loads
RDS/Aurora backup, restore, security & RDS Proxy

1. Concept

Backups restore data, security controls restrict access, and RDS Proxy lets applications reuse database connections.

2. Purpose

Restore data, protect connections and avoid database being overloaded by many connections.

3. How it works

Automated backups and snapshots

  • Automated backup combines backups with transaction logs for Point-in-Time Restore (PITR) in the retention window.
  • RDS DB instances support retention for up to 35 days; Set 0 to disable automated backup in enabled models.
  • Aurora keeps continuous backups according to configured retention.
  • Manual snapshots are kept until they are automatically deleted.
  • Restore creates a new DB/cluster, does not overwrite the running DB; must move the application to the new endpoint after checking the data.

Restore and migrate

Wrongly deleting data → PITR before the problem → new DB → check → change app endpoint
  • Some MySQL restores from S3 use backups created by Percona XtraBackup, requiring correct version/format support.
  • You don't have to upload any dump files to S3 to restore.
  • Snapshot is different from read replica: replica can replicate mistakenly deleted operations, while backup allows returning to a previous point in time.

Network, credentials and encryption

Set DB private, SG only receives from app.
TLS encryption in transit; KMS encrypts when saving and backing up.
If you want to encrypt the old database, usually snapshot → encrypted copy → new restore.
  • Secrets Manager saves/rotates passwords; IAM DB authentication uses tokens for DB users configured on the supporting engine.
  • RDS API management permissions do not automatically grant permissions to run SQL in the DB.

RDS Proxy

  • Proxy pool/reuse connection, useful when Lambda/application opens many short connections.
  • The proxy resides in the VPC, helping to reduce connection storms and improve failover handling; Does not cache SQL results or make heavy queries automatically faster.
Lambda/EC2 → RDS Proxy → connection pool → RDS/Aurora
  • Passwords can be used with Secrets Manager or IAM authentication according to supported configuration.
  • IAM is not always required for clients; End-to-end IAM can also unstore passwords in Secrets Manager. AWS RDS Proxy authentication.

Operational events and data events

  • RDS Event Notifications/EventBridge reports DB events such as failover, snapshot creation or state change; Not a stream that changes each row.
  • Some engines support calling Lambda from SQL/DB logic.
  • Requires both IAM permission and network connection to Lambda; Private DB can use NAT/VPC Appropriate endpoints.
  • If you need CDC, use the CDC tool/stream, don't rely on lifecycle notifications.

IAM Database Authentication & Cross-VPC RDS Proxy

Authentication/Connection mechanismHow it Works & Conditions
IAM Database AuthenticationAuthenticate into RDS/Aurora (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB) using Temporary auth token is valid for 15 minutes instead of static password; Request to enable features on the DB, create the corresponding DB user (eg AWSAuthenticationPlugin on MySQL), IAM permissions rds-db:connect and connect via TLS (open connection is not terminated when token expires 15 minutes)
Cross-VPC RDS Proxy EndpointAllow Lambda/EC2 in VPC A to connect to RDS Proxy and Private RDS in VPC B via ENI + Private IP (AWS PrivateLink) without needing to open a public DB or combine CIDR across the network
EC2 / Lambda (IAM Role with rds-db:connect)
  ↓ Generate IAM Auth Token (15 minutes)
  ↓ TLS connection
RDS / Aurora (reconcile DB User + IAM Token)

4. Practical example

Thousands of Lambda invocations connect through RDS Proxy to reduce direct RDS connections.

Accidentally deleted recovery: PITR. Lambda opened too many connections: RDS Proxy. DB security: private subnet + SG + TLS + KMS + secret management. Failover warning: RDS event → SNS/EventBridge.

5. Comparison

RDS Proxy manages connections, ElastiCache keeps data in memory, and PITR restores data to a point before an incident.

Amazon ElastiCache

1. Concept

ElastiCache keeps data in memory for fast access and fewer database queries.

2. Purpose

Keep frequently used data in RAM for quick response and reduce the number of database queries.

3. How it works

Cache-aside

App → cache: has data → returns results
          └─ not available → read DB → write cache with TTL → return result
  • The application must add cache read/write logic; ElastiCache does not front itself database like RDS Proxy.
  • Select TTL and invalidation to avoid returning old data for too long.
  • Write-through updates the cache at the same time as writing to the DB, making data available for reading but increasing write work and possibly caching unused data.
  • Cache-aside only loads when needed but the first read has a cache miss.

Redis/Valkey and Memcached

ChooseMain point
Redis OSS/ValkeyData structures, sorted sets, replication/read replicas, Multi-AZ failover and snapshots depending on configuration
Node-based MemcachedSimple key-value cache, sharding, multi-thread; no native replication/failover like Redis
  • Node-based Memcached does not have backup like Redis/Valkey; Serverless Memcached has snapshot/restore support.
  • So we need to look at both the engine and the deployment model. AWS backup support.

Sessions and leaderboards

  • Storing sessions in cache helps multiple app instances share the same login state.
  • Redis/Valkey sorted sets are suitable for leaderboards because they store scores and query rankings quickly.
  • Common read cache reduces repeated queries on RDS.
  • Read Replica still runs SQL on the DB data; cache stores results/objects for quick retrieval, two solutions can be used together.

Security and data may be lost

  • Use private network, source-limited SG, TLS and appropriate authentication engine/version: AUTH/RBAC or IAM authentication when supported.
  • IAM cluster management and IAM data authentication are different permissions/configurations.
  • Designed to tolerate cache misses or cache data loss; For data that needs to be sustainable, there must be a data source/backup that meets the requirements.
  • Asynchronous replication does not guarantee that all writes will not be lost in case of error.

4. Practical example

A product page reads prices and descriptions from cache; on a miss, the application reads RDS and fills the cache with a TTL.

Product catalog read a lot: cache-aside. Multiple EC2 web needs common session: Redis/Valkey. Game ranking: sorted sets. Simple, renewable cache: Memcached.

5. Comparison

A Read Replica still executes database queries; a cache stores results or objects; RDS Proxy only manages connections.

Summary

  • Amazon RDS: An AWS-managed relational database. AWS handles tasks such as backups and maintenance; you still design tables, write queries, and tune the application.
  • Multi-AZ & Read Replicas: Multi-AZ provides automatic failover. Read Replicas share read load but can lag behind the primary. Multi-AZ DB clusters have two readable readers; a DB instance standby cannot serve reads.
  • Amazon Aurora: A MySQL- or PostgreSQL-compatible database. Applications write through the writer endpoint and use the reader endpoint to share read connections across readers.
  • Aurora Serverless & Global Database: Serverless v2 adjusts compute capacity to demand. Global Database copies data to other Regions for local reads and disaster recovery.
  • Backups & RDS Proxy: Backups recover earlier data after mistakes or corruption. RDS Proxy reuses connections so the database faces fewer new connections.
  • Amazon ElastiCache: Keeps frequently used data in memory for faster responses and fewer database reads. DAX is also a cache, but specifically for DynamoDB.

Purpose-Built Databases

Amazon DocumentDB

1. Concept

DocumentDB stores records as documents with fields; records do not need exactly the same fields.

2. Purpose

Store flexible document records without operating the database cluster.

3. How it works

  • Amazon DocumentDB is a managed document database compatible with MongoDB API, used to store data in JSON document format with flexible schema.
  • DocumentDB = Document Database = MongoDB-compatible = Managed by AWS

Document Model

  • Document: A JSON-like record, such as a product's information.
  • Flexible fields: A phone has a color; a laptop has RAM and CPU fields. Both products need not have identical fields.
  • Compared with RDS/Aurora: Relational databases organize data into tables, rows, columns, and an explicit schema.
Product A
{
  name: "Phone",
  color: "Black"
}

Product B
{
  name: "Laptop",
  ram: "16GB",
  cpu: "M3"
}

MongoDB Compatibility

Applications can use:

MongoDB Driver
↓
DocumentDB
  • But remember: DocumentDB ≠ MongoDB self-hosted
  • DocumentDB = AWS service compatible with MongoDB API
  • So still need to check which MongoDB features are supported.

Managed Cluster

  • AWS admin: Provisioning · Patching · Backup · Replication · Storage scaling
  • Application still has to worry about: Document Design · Indexes · Queries · Security

Availability & Storage

DocumentDB has replication across multiple AZs:

↓

  • Objective: High Availability + Failover
  • Storage can expand automatically as data increases.

  • IAM is used to manage AWS resources, but permissions to read/write database data still require appropriate authentication/authorization mechanisms.

Document Size & DocumentDB Serverless

  • DocumentDB supports maximum document size 16 MB and there is a regime DocumentDB Serverless Self-scaling capacity every second based on DCU.

4. Practical example

A product catalog stores documents because each product type has a different set of attributes.

DocumentDB uses documents and MongoDB-compatible APIs; RDS uses SQL relations; Neptune focuses on graphs.

DocumentDB vs RDS vs Neptune

  • RDS / Aurora = SQL = relational = join multiple tables
  • DocumentDB = JSON document = flexible schema = MongoDB-compatible
  • Neptune = Graph Database = relationship / traversal

Amazon Neptune

1. Concept

Neptune stores entities and their relationships, such as people and their friendships.

2. Purpose

Query multi-hop relationships between entities using a graph-oriented data model.

3. How it works

  • Amazon Neptune is a managed graph database used when data has many relationships and connections between them need to be queried.
  • Neptune = Graph Database = Node + Relationship

Graph Model

  • Node/Vertex: An entity such as a person, account, or device.
  • Edge: A relationship between entities, such as friendship or shared device use.
  • Neptune: Suits queries traversing many relationships, such as finding connected account groups.
User A
↓ FRIEND_OF
User B
↓ BOUGHT

Neptune is strong when the question needs to go through many relationships.

Availability

  • AWS admin: Cluster · Replication · Backup · Failover
  • Read replicas can be used to scale reading workload.

Neptune Streams

  • Neptune Streams = records changes in the graph sequentially.
Graph changes

No need to poll the entire graph to find out what just changed.

Graph databases prioritize relationship traversal; document databases organize documents; relational databases use tables and joins.

Neptune vs Other Databases

Amazon Timestream

1. Concept

Timestream stores time-stamped data, such as sensor temperatures each minute, for analysis over time.

2. Purpose

Store and analyze timestamped measurements such as metrics and telemetry.

3. How it works

  • This topic describes Timestream for LiveAnalytics, closed to new customers since June 20, 2025 while supporting existing customers.
  • Timestream for InfluxDB is a separate option with a different deployment model.
  • Amazon Timestream is a serverless time-series database used to store and analyze timestamp data.
  • Timestream = Time Series Database = Timestamp + Measurements

Time Series Data

For example:

10:00 → CPU = 40%
10:01 → CPU = 55%
10:02 → CPU = 80%
  • Or IoT: Timestamp · DeviceId · Temperature · Humidity · Battery

Time is the most important part of data.

Data Model

  • A record usually has: Timestamp + Dimensions + Measures
  • Timestamp = 10:00 · Dimensions: · deviceId = sensor-01 · region = ap-southeast-1 · Measures: · temperature = 30 · humidity = 70

Multi-measure Record

If multiple measurements have the same timestamp:

  • 10:00 · Temperature = 30 · Humidity = 70 · Battery = 90%
  • Can be saved together: 1 Timestamp + many Measures
  • → reduce the number of records/writes.

Amazon Keyspaces (for Apache Cassandra)

1. Concept

Keyspaces is a Cassandra-compatible wide-column database whose underlying servers are managed by AWS.

2. Purpose

Run Cassandra-compatible workloads without managing cluster nodes and capacity.

3. How it works

  • Run an Apache Cassandra compatible wide-column database in fully serverless mode on AWS.

Architecture, Capacity & Integration Mode

AspectFeatures on Amazon Keyspaces
Platform architectureNoSQL managed, CQL-compatible serverless Apache Cassandra; automatically cloned via 3 Availability Zones with single-digit millisecond latency
On-Demand ModePay according to each actual read/write request, suitable for unpredictable load fluctuations
Provisioned Mode (+ Auto Scaling)Preset read/write capacity for stable load to optimize costs
Data Protection & CDCDefault encryption (AWS Owned Key or CMK), PITR Continuous recovery in 35 days and CDC Streams push record changes to Lambda, EventBridge, or Kinesis

4. Practical example

An application using Cassandra Query Language moves to Keyspaces to reduce node-management work.

5. Comparison

Keyspaces suits Cassandra applications; DynamoDB has its own APIs and access model.

Summary

  • Amazon DocumentDB: Stores flexible documents through MongoDB-compatible APIs. Useful when records do not all share the same fields.
  • Amazon Neptune: Stores and queries relationships, such as who knows whom or which accounts share a device.
  • Amazon Timestream: Stores time-based measurements such as temperature or metrics. LiveAnalytics is closed to new customers; InfluxDB is a separate option.
  • Amazon Keyspaces: Runs a Cassandra-compatible database without managing a cluster. Existing Cassandra applications should verify compatibility before migrating.

Route 53 & Hybrid DNS

DNS & Amazon Route 53 fundamentals

1. Concept

Route 53 is a DNS service that helps applications find connection destinations from domain names such as example.com.

2. Purpose

Use easy-to-remember domain names instead of IPs and manage how DNS returns application endpoints.

3. How it works

How does DNS work?

  • DNS translates a name like api.example.com to an IP or destination name.
  • Resolver asks name servers when there is no cache; The authoritative name server is where the official answer for the zone is located.
Client → DNS resolver → Root → TLD (.com) → authoritative server
       ← IP is cached according to TTL
Client → connects directly to the application
  • Route 53: managed authoritative DNS, supporting domain registration and health-aware routing.
  • It returns a DNS answer, not a proxy web request.

TTL

  • TTL: How long a DNS answer may remain cached.
  • High TTL: Fewer DNS queries, but slower propagation of address changes to users.
  • Low TTL: Faster updates at the cost of more DNS queries.
  • A record: Includes its name, type, value/target, TTL where applicable, and routing policy.
  • Remember: DNS changes do not close existing connections or immediately clear every cache.

Public and Private Hosted Zones

  • Public zones serve public domain names on the Internet.
  • Private zone serving VPC is associated and hybrid network through appropriate Resolver; private names do not automatically open network access to the application.
  • To get private DNS working in VPC, enable the necessary DNS properties and check the association.
  • A hosted zone only contains records, not where the website is stored.

Registrar is different from DNS service

  • You can buy a domain from another registrar and then use Route 53: create a public hosted zone, update the name servers in the registrar according to the zone's NS.
  • It is not required to transfer domain ownership to AWS.

Route 53 A Alias Record Support Destination

  • Route 53 Alias Record Can be used right away zone apex (example.com — where not created CNAME) and freely point the query to two target groups: (1) AWS Resources is supported (ALB/NLB, CloudFront, API Gateway, S3 Website Endpoint, Global Accelerator, Elastic Beanstalk, VPC Interface Endpoint) and (2) another DNS Record of the same type in the same Hosted Zone.

4. Practical example

shop.example.com resolves to an ALB, while a database has an internal name in a private hosted zone.

Root domain pointing to ALB: Alias. Internal name for database/service: Private Hosted Zone. Public domain purchased outside of AWS: delegate DNS to Route 53.

5. Comparison

DNS answers where to connect; a load balancer distributes the resulting connections or requests.

Records need to be distinguished

RecordMeaning
AName → IPv4
AAAAName → IPv6
CNAMEName → other name; Do not use at zone apex
NSName server is responsible for zone/delegation
Alias of Route 53Point to a supporting AWS resource or appropriate record in the zone; Can be used at apex

For example example.com → ALB uses Alias; www.example.com can use CNAME.

Alias does not arbitrarily point to every hostname outside of AWS like CNAME and is not a direct way to point to EC2's IP using Alias.

Route 53 routing policies

1. Concept

A routing policy determines which destination Route 53 returns to a DNS query.

2. Purpose

Select endpoints returned in DNS by rollout rate, latency, location or health.

3. How it works

DNS Flow:

Client
↓ DNS query
Route 53 routing policy
↓ returns the appropriate endpoint
Client connects to endpoint

Simple

  • A record has one or more values; The client chooses when receiving multiple values.
  • Simple does not attach health checks directly to records; Alias ​​has Evaluate Target Health according to supported target types.
  • Suitable for a simple endpoint, no need for complex selection policies.

Weighted

  • Weight: Assigned to records sharing a name and type; weights need not total 100.
  • Example: Weights of 1 and 9 give roughly 10%/90% of DNS answers. Caching prevents exact request-level proportions.
  • Weight zero: Normally excluded while healthy positive-weight records exist; may be considered if those records are unhealthy.
  • All zero: Answers are shared equally. Zero weight is not an access-blocking mechanism.

Latency-based

  • Choose the Region with the best measured latency for the query side, not necessarily the closest by geographical distance.
  • Suitable for apps running multiple regions that need quick response, can combine health to eliminate error endpoints.

Failover

  • Active-passive: if primary is healthy, return primary; If primary fails, switch to secondary according to configured health.
  • Suitable for DR site or backup notification site.

Geolocation

  • Select by queryer's location: continent, country or US state.
  • More specific regions are preferred; create default record for unmatched location.
  • Content/language matching by country.
  • DNS routing is not a mechanism to prevent unauthorized access; want to enforce permissions/areas that need to be controlled in the application/edge.

Geoproximity

  • Choose according to the distance between user and resource, with bias to expand/retract service area.
  • Positive bias increases resource traffic, negative bias decreases.
  • Other geolocation is divided by national borders and latency is selected by latency.

IP-based

  • Use CIDR collection to map the query source/client address provided by DNS to the location/endpoint.
  • Suitable when you know the company's/ISP's IP range and want your own route;
  • The DNS resolver and EDNS client subnet can affect the addresses Route 53 sees.

Multi-Value Answer

  • Return up to 8 healthy records per query when configuring health check.
  • The client connects itself to a value; There is no connection management proxy like ELB.

4. Practical example

A team uses weighted routing to test a new version with a portion of traffic before increasing its share.

Canary about 10%: Weighted. Global app prioritizes latency: Latency-based. By language/country: Geolocation. Change service area by bias: Geoproximity. Primary/backup: Failover.

5. Comparison

Weighted uses weights; latency routing prioritizes latency; geolocation uses location; failover chooses primary or backup.

Route 53 health checks & failover

1. Concept

Health checks test destination health so Route 53 can select an appropriate DNS answer under its routing policy.

2. Purpose

Use health checks to select healthy endpoints and switch to a backup location when a failure occurs.

3. How it works

Three ways to determine health

WayWhen used
Endpoint health checkPublic HTTP/HTTPS/TCP endpoint
Calculated health checkCombine the results of multiple health check children
CloudWatch alarm health checkMetric/alarm signal, useful for private resources
Route 53 health checker on the Internet does not go directly to the internal private IP
VPC.
With private apps, you can use monitoring inside VPC → metric/alarm → health
check.

Evaluate Target Health

  • Alias to supported resources such as ELBs can obtain the target's health using Evaluate Target Health, without creating a separate Route 53 endpoint check.
  • Not all alias targets support this; CloudFront does not support Evaluate Target Health = Yes.
  • Check the health of a specific endpoint, avoid checking the domain itself that is failing over because the results will depend on its DNS routing. AWS health-check configuration.

Active-passive failover

Primary healthy → DNS returns primary
Primary unhealthy → DNS returns secondary according to configured health
  • Primary needs appropriate health signal: health check or Evaluate Target Health with Alias support.
  • Secondary must be truly ready to serve and have data/capacity that meets DR.

DNS has cache

  • Failover DNS only changes the answer for the next query; Do not transfer current connection open.
  • TTL, error detection time, and client retry all affect interruption time paragraph.
  • If all endpoints are unhealthy, Route 53 has fail-open behavior depending on the policy; Do not consider health check as a firewall that always blocks all faulty endpoints.

4. Practical example

When the primary Region's application fails, DNS directs users to a prepared application in another Region.

Active-passive between two Regions; primary ALB and static recovery page; Calculated checks reflect multiple dependencies. Try actual DR, checking both DNS and database and application status.

5. Comparison

DNS failover changes DNS answers; database failover changes database processing; each needs its own design.

Hybrid DNS & Route 53 Resolver

1. Concept

Route 53 Resolver enables private DNS resolution between VPCs and corporate networks.

2. Purpose

For AWS and on-premises to resolve each other's private domain names.

3. How it works

Resolver in VPC

  • Route 53 Resolver handles AWS-provided names, public DNS and associated Private Hosted Zone.
  • VPN/Direct Connect connection creates a network connection but does not automatically make all private DNS on both sides understand each other.

Inbound endpoint

  • Inbound: query direction going into AWS: DNS on-prem forward domain AWS/private zone to the IP of the inbound endpoint.
On-prem machine → DNS on-prem → VPN/DX → Inbound endpoint → Private Hosted Zone
  • There is no need to turn private records into public for internal users to look up.

Outbound endpoint

  • Outbound: Query direction going out of AWS: Resolver forwarding rule selects suffix like corp.example.com and then sends query through outbound endpoint to DNS on-prem.
EC2 → VPC Resolver → rule for corp.example.com → Outbound → DNS on-prem
  • The rule must be associated with the VPC in use; can share cross-account rules via AWS RAM.

Availability and network

  • Use IP endpoints in at least two AZs, SGs for DNS TCP/UDP 53 and VPN/DX lines to the DNS server.
  • Check route/firewall both ways.
  • Do not forward the same domain around to both sides and then back to yourself.
  • Resolvable DNS still needs route/SG to connect to the return IP.

Three types of Resolver Rule: Forward Rule, System Rule & Delegation Rule

Ingredients / RulesDistinguishing function
Inbound EndpointReceive DNS queries from On-premises going into VPC Resolver (Private Hosted Zone resolution)
Outbound Endpoint + Forward RulePass the DNS query of the specified domain (eg corp.example.com) from VPC to DNS Server On-premises IP
System RuleKeep a specific subdomain (eg aws.corp.example.com) back to AWS VPC Resolver to process, causing an exception to override the parent Forward Rule
Delegation RuleAuthorize subdomain resolution based on records NS to the authoritative DNS server via Outbound Endpoint

4. Practical example

Office servers resolve a private AWS database name through a Resolver inbound endpoint.

On-prem look up the private database name in AWS: Inbound. EC2 looks up Active Directory domain on-prem: Outbound + forwarding rule. Multiple accounts share the same hybrid DNS: share rules.

5. Comparison

Inbound endpoints receive DNS queries into AWS; outbound endpoints forward VPC queries to external DNS using rules.

Summary

  • Amazon Route 53: DNS resolves names so clients can find applications. Public hosted zones serve public names; private hosted zones serve private networks.
  • Routing Policies: Choose DNS answers by need: traffic share, latency, location, or failover to a backup destination.
  • Health Checks & Failover: Checks endpoints to direct DNS toward healthy destinations. Switching is not instant because clients may cache earlier answers.
  • Route 53 Resolver: Inbound endpoints receive company-network DNS queries into AWS. Outbound endpoints forward AWS queries to company DNS using configured rules.

Amazon S3

Amazon S3 fundamentals, buckets & websites

1. Concept

S3 stores files as objects in buckets; each object has a key used to locate and access it.

2. Purpose

Store and distribute files, backups, logs, and lake data at scale without managing disks.

3. How it works

  • Amazon S3 is an object storage service for storing files, backups, logs, data lakes and static content with very high durability and scalability.
  • S3 = Object Storage = File / Backup / Log / Data Lake

Bucket & Object

S3 saves data according to:

Bucket
↓
Object
  • Objects include: Key · Value (data/file) · Metadata

For example:

  • Bucket: company-data · images/logo.png · logs/2026/app.log · reports/report.pdf
  • images/ good logs/ just prefix logic, not the real filesystem folder.

Bucket = container, Object = file/data, Key = name/path of object.

Bucket & Region

  • Bucket is created in an AWS Region.
S3 Bucket
↓
ap-southeast-1
  • Bucket names according to the S3 namespace must normally be unique.
  • Some important rules: 3–63 characters · lowercase · no underscore _ · not the same as IP address

S3 looks like a global service, but bucket's data is in the selected Region.

Object Size

  • Object: A file and its metadata stored in S3; each object can be up to 50 TB.
  • Multipart Upload: Splits a large file into parts for parallel uploads; S3 assembles the parts into one object.
Large File
↓
divided into many parts
↓
parallel upload
↓
S3 reassembled

Consistency

  • S3 provided strong consistency for: PUT · DELETE · LIST
Upload objects
↓
GET/LIST immediately after
↓
see new version

S3 is not a File System

  • S3 is not: EBS = block storage · EFS = file system
  • The application accesses S3 via: API · SDK · HTTP · AWS CLI

Static Website Hosting

  • S3 can host: HTML · CSS · JavaScript · Images

But S3 website endpoint mainly uses:

HTTP

Production is usually better:

User
↓ HTTPS
CloudFront
↓
Private S3 Bucket
  • with: OAC = Origin Access Control
  • → S3 does not need to be published directly.

S3 Security

  • There are two common classes of permissions: IAM Policy = What can principal do?
  • Bucket Policy = who can access bucket/object
IAM User
↓
IAM Allow s3:GetObject
↓
S3

or cross-account:

Account A
↓
Bucket Policy
↓
Bucket Account B
  • Principle: Explicit Deny · > all Allows

Block Public Access

  • This is a guardrail against accidentally publicizing a bucket/object.
  • Block Public Access = block public access
  • If you want to static public website directly using S3, you must configure public access accordingly.
  • Production often prioritizes: Private S3 + CloudFront

Object Ownership & ACL

  • Modern S3 often uses: Bucket Owner Enforced
  • → ACL is disabled.
  • Main rights are governed by: IAM Policy · Bucket Policy · Access Point Policy

Prioritize policies instead of ACLs.

Pre-Signed URL

  • Used to give users access temporary private object.

Download:

Upload:

↓
Browser
↓

  • Object still: Private ✅
  • The URL is only valid for the specified time.

Pre-signed URL ≠ public bucket.

S3 Access Points

  • Access Point creates separate endpoint + policy for each application/team.
An S3 Bucket

Can create:

  • → avoid a policy bucket that is too large.

You can limit access only from VPC:

↓

  • Suitable when you want: S3 access · does not go to the Internet

S3 Object Lambda

  • Allowed transform object at GET without modifying the original object.
Application
↓

↓

↓
Transform
↓
Response
Redact PII
Resize image
Add watermark
XML → JSON
  • Original Object: no change

4. Practical example

A website stores product images in S3 and delivers them through CloudFront.

Backup / Archive · Logs · Data Lake · Images / Videos · Static Assets · Software Distribution · Disaster Recovery

Commonly used long-term archives: S3 Glacier storage classes

5. Comparison

S3 is object storage; EBS provides block disks; EFS provides a shared filesystem.

S3 encryption, HTTPS & CORS

1. Concept

Encryption protects data, HTTPS protects it in transit, and CORS controls whether a different website can read a response in the browser.

2. Purpose

Protect content stored in S3, encrypt transmission and let the browser access the correct origin.

3. How it works

Encryption at rest

WayWho manages the key?Point to understand
SSE-S3S3/AWSAES-256 server-side encryption; default for new objects
SSE-KMSAWS KMSControl key policy, audit key usage, have KMS permission/quota/cost
SSE-CCustomersSend key per request over HTTPS; S3 does not save keys for future use
Client-sideApplication/clientEncrypt before upload; S3 only receives ciphertext
  • Encryption does not grant access on its own and does not change bucket policy.
  • Changing the default encryption does not automatically re-encrypt any old objects with the new type/key.

SSE-KMS

  • Upload needs appropriate permissions like kms:GenerateDataKey; reading requires kms:Decrypt.
  • Cross-account access requires S3 permission and customer managed KMS key permission from the other side.
  • KMS's request rate may become limited when S3 traffic is large.
  • S3 Bucket Keys help reduce KMS requests and SSE-KMS costs when appropriate; must still keep the correct key policy.

HTTPS

  • TLS/HTTPS protects transmission. Bucket policy can be explicit Deny if aws:SecureTransport=false to deny HTTP.
Client --HTTPS--> S3 --server-side encryption--> object is saved
  • S3 website endpoint does not support HTTPS directly; Use CloudFront for your website HTTPS. SSE-C requires HTTPS and must keep the key secure. If the key is lost, it cannot be decrypted.

CORS

  • Origin includes protocol, host and port.
  • The web at https://app.example.com calling S3 at another origin needs CORS on the destination bucket with the appropriate origin/method/header.
  • CORS is a browser rule for reading responses, does not grant S3 permissions and does not prevent any clients outside the browser.
  • Pre-signed URL/bucket policy must still allow action first.

4. Practical example

A browser uploads images over HTTPS using presigned URLs and CORS allowing the website's domain.

Need control/audit key: SSE-KMS. Browser uploads directly to S3: pre-signed PUT + CORS. Data must be encrypted before reaching S3: client-side. Only for secure transmission: policy enforces HTTPS.

5. Comparison

CORS does not grant S3 permission; IAM and bucket policies do; SSE-KMS also requires KMS key permissions.

S3 versioning, replication & immutable protection

1. Concept

Versioning keeps older versions, replication copies objects, and Object Lock enforces configured retention against unauthorized deletion or changes.

2. Purpose

Recover an overwritten/wrongly deleted object that has a copy elsewhere and keep the data from being destroyed prematurely.

3. How it works

Three different mechanisms:

  • Versioning = keeping versions
  • Replication = copy to another bucket
  • Object Lock = version protection by retention

Versioning

  • Versioning: Keeps several versions of the same file in a bucket. Overwriting the file creates a new version.
  • Normal deletion: S3 adds a delete marker; older versions remain available for recovery.
  • Recovery: Retrieve an older version or remove the appropriate delete marker to make the file visible again.
  • Permanent deletion: Deleting a specific version ID removes that version. Suspending versioning does not remove existing versions.

CRR and SRR

  • CRR replicate to another Region; SRR replicates in the same Region.
  • Can cross-account, needs Versioning at source/destination with appropriate roles and policies; The SSE-KMS object also needs KMS configuration/permissions.
  • Asynchronous replication: does not promise data to appear immediately.
  • By default, the live replication rule applies new objects after enabling it; old object used S3 Batch Replication.

Delete and replicate

  • Delete markers can be replicated if appropriate options and rules are enabled.
  • Permanently deleting a version at the source does not automatically replicate that deletion to the destination.
There is no automatic chaining: object replicate A → B does not continue on its own to B → C
just because B has a rule to C.
If you want multiple destinations, configure appropriate replication from the source.

MFA Delete

  • MFA required when permanently deleting version or changing Versioning status.
  • MFA is not required just to add a regular delete marker.
  • Bucket owner/root enables or disables MFA Delete via API/CLI with MFA, not via S3 Console. Versioning must be enabled.

Object Lock

  • WORM protects each version; Need Versioning.
  • Can be enabled for new or existing buckets according to the support process; Once enabled, do not turn off Object Lock/Versioning in the usual way.
MechanismMeaning
Governance retentionPeople with appropriate bypass permissions can bypass protection
Compliance retentionNo one, not even root, can remove/shorten the retention of the locked version
Legal holdHold until the person with the right to remove the hold; independent of retention date
  • Locking an existing version still allows writing a new version with the same key.
  • Legal hold does not expire; Expired retention may not be deleted if legal hold is still on.

4. Practical example

A company retains records with Object Lock and replicates them to a backup Region.

File is overwritten: Versioning. DR/compliance multiple Region: CRR. Anti-ransomware backups or records should remain intact: Object Lock, limited permissions and appropriate policy retention.

5. Comparison

Replication does not replace point-in-time backups; versioning alone does not prevent authorized permanent deletion.

S3 storage classes & lifecycle

1. Concept

Storage classes match storage to access needs; lifecycle rules automatically transition or delete objects.

2. Purpose

Choose the appropriate storage class to optimize costs based on:

3. How it works

  • Access frequency · Retrieval time · AZ number · Retention time · Ability to reproduce data

Overview of Storage Classes

  • S3 Standard = frequent visits
  • Standard-IA = less access = multi-AZ
  • One Zone-IA = low traffic = 1 AZ
  • Intelligent-Tiering = AWS automatically changes tiers according to access patterns
  • Glacier = archive / long-term storage
  • Express One Zone = 1 AZ = very low latency / high performance
  • Common points: Durability = 99.999999999% (11 nines)
  • Main differences: Availability · AZ · Storage cost · Retrieval cost · Retrieval time · Minimum storage duration

S3 Standard

  • S3 Standard = general purpose = frequent access = multi-AZ = low latency = high throughput
  • No: Retrieval fee · Minimum storage duration
  • Suitable: Active application data · Web content · Big Data · Mobile / Gaming

Standard-IA

  • IA = Infrequent Access

Used for data:

Less access
but when needed
→ must get it immediately
  • Standard-IA = multi-AZ = fast retrieval = cheaper storage Standard = has a retrieval fee
  • According to the slides: Minimum storage duration = 30 days

Use cases:

  • Backups · Disaster Recovery · Data is rarely accessed

One Zone-IA

  • One Zone-IA = same as IA but only stored in 1 AZ

Therefore:

Cheaper than Standard-IA
but
AZ is lost
→ data may be lost
  • Suitable when data: Can be recreated · or · already has another copy

For example:

  • Thumbnails · Secondary backup · Re-creatable data

Test score: need infrequent access + data can recreate → think about One Zone-IA.

S3 Intelligent-Tiering

  • Use when: Unknown · access pattern
  • AWS automatically tracks access and transfers objects between tiers.
Frequent Access
↓ no access for 30 days
Infrequent Access
↓ No longer access
Archive Instant Access
  • There are additional optional archive tiers: Archive Access · Deep Archive Access
  • Advantages: Automatically optimize storage cost · No need to manually write transitions according to access pattern · No retrieval fee between access tiers
  • Yes: Monitoring / automation fee

Test score: access pattern is unstable or unpredictable → Intelligent-Tiering.

S3 Glacier Classes

  • Used for: Archive · Backup · Long-term retention
1. Glacier Instant Retrieval
  • Retrieval = milliseconds
  • Archive is rarely accessed · but when needed, get it immediately
  • Minimum storage = 90 days
2. Glacier Flexible Retrieval
  • Retrieval can choose: Expedited = 1–5 minutes
  • Standard = 3–5 hours
  • Bulk = 5–12 hours
  • Archive is appropriate when: No need to get it right away
3. Glacier Deep Archive
  • Cheapest + retrieval is the slowest
  • Standard ≈ 12 hours
  • Bulk ≈ 48 hours
  • Minimum storage = 180 days
  • Compliance archive · Long-term backup · Data for many years is rarely accessed

Glacier storage classes

Instant
= milliseconds

Flexible
= minutes → hours

Deep Archive
= many hours
= cheapest
  • Minimum storage according to slide: Instant = 90 days
  • Flexible = 90 days
  • Deep Archive = 180 days

S3 Express One Zone

  • Express One Zone = high-performance storage class = single AZ
  • Object is saved in: Directory Bucket
  • Characteristics: Very low Latency · High request throughput · Compute + storage can colocate in the same AZ
  • AI / ML · HPC · Financial modeling · Media processing · Latency-sensitive workloads
  • Points to remember: Express One Zone = performance
  • One Zone-IA = save costs for rarely accessed data
  • Both are: Single AZ; but use cases are different.

  • DLM manages EBS backups, not the lifecycle of S3 objects, even though EBS snapshots are stored by AWS in S3 on the service side.

S3 events & service integrations

1. Concept

S3 sends notifications about object changes, such as triggering thumbnail creation after an image upload.

2. Purpose

Automatically handle new or changed objects, instead of constantly checking the bucket.

3. How it works

Event stream

Upload object → S3 event → SQS / SNS / Lambda / EventBridge
                           └─ processor reads object from bucket
  • Event describes the bucket/key/version and operation, not the entire file content.
  • Prefix/suffix can be filtered to only process uploads/ or .jpg.

Select destination

  • SQS serves as a buffer for workers; SNS fan-out; Lambda processing directly; EventBridge has rules/filters and many target types.
  • The direct SQS/SNS/Lambda destination must be in the same Region as the bucket according to the supported configuration.
  • S3 does not send directly to the SQS FIFO; Use EventBridge if you need a FIFO target. AWS notification destinations.

Where is Permission located?

  • Notification permission: The SQS, SNS, or Lambda resource policy must allow the S3 service (s3.amazonaws.com) to send notifications to it.
  • Source restriction: Allow only the required bucket ARN and AWS account. A destination encrypted with KMS also needs key permissions.
  • File access: The consumer needs permission to read the object and decrypt it if necessary. Permission to receive events does not grant file access.
  • Relevant APIs: SQS uses sqs:SendMessage; SNS uses sns:Publish; Lambda uses lambda:InvokeFunction.

Duplicate events and loops

  • Events can repeat: A notification may arrive more than once, and there is no global event order.
  • Avoid duplicate effects: Processing the same object/version again must not repeat the business action, such as sending two emails for one upload.
  • Avoid loops: Separate input and output prefixes or buckets. A thumbnail Lambda should not write back into the location that triggers itself.

4. Practical example

A new bucket image produces an SQS message; workers generate thumbnails and handle retries.

Upload photo → thumbnail. New file → SQS → worker processing with retry. Event by object type → EventBridge → Step Functions for multiple steps.

5. Comparison

Event notifications trigger processing; replication copies data; inventory periodically lists objects.

S3 performance, Requester Pays & Batch Operations

1. Concept

S3 supports faster transfers, operations across many objects, and requester-paid access charges.

2. Purpose

Speed up file transfers, manipulate multiple objects, and choose which side pays the cost of reading data.

3. How it works

Tools according to your needs:

  • Multipart Upload = split file into multiple parts
  • Byte-range GET = read part of object
  • Transfer Acceleration = transfer acceleration across the edge
  • Batch Operations = process multiple objects
  • Requester Pays = requester pays request/data transfer fee

Request rate by prefix

  • S3 supports at least 3,500 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE and 5,500 GET/HEAD per second per partitioned prefix.
  • Distributing workload across multiple prefixes helps increase total throughput; When scaling quickly, you may encounter 503 Slow Down and need to retry/backoff.
  • Prefix: the beginning of the object key, not the physical folder.
  • With SSE-KMS, the KMS request quota must also be considered.

Multipart Upload

  • Split object into parts to upload in parallel; If the part is defective, just send that part back.
  • Usually worth using from about 100 MB, required when exceeding the 5 GB single PUT limit.
  • The maximum object is now 50 TB via multipart, instead of 5 TB in the old document.
  • Configure lifecycle cleaning incomplete multipart upload to avoid unfinished part fees. AWS object upload limits.

Transfer Acceleration and byte-range GET

  • Transfer Acceleration receives data at the edge location and then uses the AWS network to reach the bucket, allowing uploading from remote locations via the Internet; must turn on the bucket and measure the benefits versus costs.
  • Multipart combinations are possible.
  • Byte-range GET takes part of an object or multiple ranges in parallel, reading header/file chunks and speeding up download; error just retry the corresponding part.

Batch Operations

  • A job includes a manifest object, action, and IAM role.
  • Used to copy, change tags, restore archive or invoke Lambda on many objects; service tracks and generates completion reports.
  • Use Inventory/Athena to select objects into the manifest when appropriate.
  • Changing the old encryption object usually requires copy/rewrite, not just changing the default encryption in the bucket.

Requester Pays

  • Authenticated requesters pay request and download fees; The bucket owner still pays for storage.
  • Requester must confirm payment; anonymous access is not supported.
  • This is a fee mechanism and does not grant permission to read the bucket. Still need policy to allow requests.

Parallel download using S3 Byte-Range Fetches

S3 Large Object
  ├─ HTTP Range: bytes=0-99MB → Worker 1
  ├─ HTTP Range: bytes=100-199MB → Worker 2
  └─ HTTP Range: bytes=200-299MB → Worker 3
  ↓ Merge order at client → Increase download throughput & retry only error parts

4. Practical example

A team retags millions of objects with Batch Operations instead of scripting individual updates.

Large video: multipart. Upload from multiple continents: try Transfer Acceleration. Retag millions of objects: Batch Operations. Divide large dataset to another account: Requester Pays.

5. Comparison

Multipart divides uploads; byte-range GET divides downloads; Batch Operations processes lists of objects.

S3 logging, Storage Lens & analytics

1. Concept

S3 logs and reports show access activity, storage usage, and optimization opportunities.

2. Purpose

Know who is accessing it, how much data is being used and where costs can be reduced or protection increased.

3. How it works

Choose tools according to the question:

  • Server Access Logging = request to bucket
  • CloudTrail data events = object-level API activity
  • Storage Lens = usage statistics / configuration
  • Storage Class Analysis = suggested lifecycle

Choose tools according to the question

ToolsAnswer the question

S3 Inventory

List of objects/versions and metadata for analysis or batch
  • CloudTrail management events do not replace object data events; You need to enable the appropriate data event type to audit object operations and consider costs.

4. Practical example

After a file is deleted, the security team uses CloudTrail data events to identify the API caller.
