AWS Backup flashboard — SAA-C03

1. Concept

Backup mechanisms protect different data types; choose the right retention and recovery method.

2. Purpose

Compare backup and data protection mechanisms to choose the right recovery based on service, timing, and retention.

3. How it works

What data to backup?

Service / mechanismDataFunction / when selected
RDS Automated BackupsRDS databaseAutomatic backup + transaction logs; PITR support up to 35 days when enabled
Aurora Automated BackupsAurora clusterContinuous, incremental backup; PITR up to 35 days; always on
RDS / Aurora Manual SnapshotDatabaseCopy at a time, keep until actively deleted
Amazon Data Lifecycle Manager (DLM)EBS volumes / EBS-backed AMIsCreate, keep, and delete scheduled EBS snapshots/AMIs
EBS SnapshotEBS volumeBackup incremental; restore to new EBS volume
AWS BackupEBS, RDS, Aurora, EFS, DynamoDB, FSx, S3…Centralized management: schedule, retention, cross-Region/cross-account copy when resources support
AWS Backup Continuous BackupSupport resources like RDS, Aurora, S3PITR in recovery window is configured; Does not apply to all resources
S3 VersioningS3 objectsKeep multiple versions; Recover when overwritten/wrongly deleted if the required version is still available
S3 Replication (SRR/CRR)S3 objectsCopy to bucket of same Region or different Region
S3 Object LockS3 object versionsWORM: prevent version deletion/editing in retention; governance has appropriate bypass permissions
DynamoDB PITRDynamoDB tableContinuous backup; restore back in time within a maximum of 35 days to a new table
DynamoDB On-Demand BackupDynamoDB tableCreate full backups when needed, keep until deleted
EFS Automatic BackupEFS file systemAWS Backup automatically backs up according to the configured policy
  • Replication can propagate changes/deletions according to configuration, so backup/version history is still needed to go back to before the error.
  • Object Lock protects versions; Do not create an independent backup yourself.

Retention and characteristics

Backup typeRetention / characteristics
RDS AutomatedDB instance: 0–35 days, 0 is off; Multi-AZ DB cluster: 1–35 days
Aurora Automated1–35 days; Automated backups cannot be turned off
DynamoDB PITRRecovery window 1–35 days, default 35 when enabled
RDS / Aurora Manual SnapshotKeep until deleted
EBS SnapshotIncremental; Keep until deleted or processed by lifecycle policy
AWS Backup ContinuousPITR up to 35 days; depending on the configured service and retention
AWS Backup PeriodicRetention according to backup plan; Supports long-term storage, cold storage capabilities depending on resources
  • AWS Backup continuous supports RDS instances/Aurora accordingly, but does not support RDS Multi-AZ DB clusters.
  • Copy continuous backup to another Region/account to create a snapshot copy, without keeping the PITR window of the source copy.

· · ·

4. Practical example

After accidental table deletion, the team selects a pre-incident recovery point and restores to a new resource for validation.

  • Wrongly deleting database data: PITR forward error if still in recovery window.
  • Backup EBS daily and keep for 7 days: DLM; Many services/accounts need common management: AWS Backup.
  • Save database copies for many months/years: manual snapshot or periodic backup with appropriate retention.
  • S3 overwritten: restore old version; Need to prevent version deletion according to retention: Object Lock with appropriate mode.

5. Comparison

Backups retain recovery points; archives prioritize long retention; replication maintains copies; DR covers both data and applications.

Summary

  • Disaster Recovery: RPO is acceptable data loss; RTO is acceptable recovery time. Faster recovery usually needs more standby infrastructure and higher cost.
  • Elastic Disaster Recovery: Continuously copies server data to AWS for recovery instances during a disaster. Networking, configuration, and recovery testing are still required.
  • AWS DMS & SCT: DMS moves database data and can keep copying changes during migration. SCT helps assess and convert schemas when changing database engines.
  • AWS Backup: Manages backup schedules and retention across services. Vault Lock protects retention; restore tests confirm backups are usable.
  • Discovery & Application Migration: Discovery inventories servers and application dependencies. MGN replicates and migrates servers to EC2 through a tested cutover plan.
  • Hybrid Migration: VM Import brings VM images into AWS. MGN migrates running servers. File and bulk-data transfers need suitable data-transfer tools instead.
  • Backup, Replication & Recovery: Backups retain earlier recovery points. Replication maintains copies but can also copy accidental deletions. DR restores both applications and data.

Well-Architected & Trusted Advisor

AWS Well-Architected Framework & Tool

1. Concept

Well-Architected provides questions and principles for reviewing architecture; the Tool records results, risks, and improvements.

2. Purpose

AWS Well-Architected Framework helps evaluate architecture according to best practices instead of just looking at individual services. AWS Well-Architected Tool turns that review process into trackable workloads, questions, risks, and improvement plans.

3. How it works

6 pillars

  • Operational excellence: Run systems well through monitoring, automation, and continual improvement.
  • Security: Protect identities, data, and systems; grant only the access needed.
  • Reliability: Keep applications working through failures and recover after incidents.
  • Performance efficiency: Choose the right resource types and amounts to meet performance needs.
  • Cost optimization: Meet requirements at a reasonable cost and avoid wasted resources.
  • Sustainability: Use resources efficiently to reduce environmental impact.
  • Application: Consider all six together; one change can improve several pillars or require balancing them.

Workload review

  • Workload: An application or business process made of resources and code, such as a shopping website or order processing system.
  • Review: Use structured questions to identify architectural strengths, risks, and improvements.
  • Tool's role: Records answers and tracks decisions; it does not fix the system automatically or replace architectural judgment.
Define workload
↓
Select lens
↓
Answer review questions
↓
Identify high / medium risks
↓
Improvement plan

AWS Well-Architected Tool

  • Well-Architected Tool is a free service in AWS Console to review workloads according to 6 pillars and related lenses.
Console
↓
AWS Well-Architected Tool
↓
Workloads
↓
Reports / Dashboard / Improvement plan
  • Common process: Select workload · Answer questions · Review results by pillar · Generate report · Track improvement status
  • Dashboard displays total workload and number of high/medium risk issues; Report helps share results with people who are not directly accessing the tool. AWS WA Tool

Access and scope

  • IAM controls who can create, view, edit, or share review workloads.
  • Review answers are architectural information, so do not enter unnecessary secrets, passwords or sensitive data.
  • The tool mainly works on metadata and review responses; it does not install agents into your workload and does not grant data-plane permissions to your application.
  • Console/API access using TLS;
  • The power to improve the actual architecture still lies with the target services such as IAM, VPC, RDS, S3 or CloudFormation.

4. Practical example

Before expanding production, a team reviews its workload against six pillars and tracks outstanding improvements.

  • Review a workload before production launch to find high risk issues regarding security, reliability or cost.
  • Track architectural improvements each sprint using improvement plans and milestone/report.
  • Standardize reviews of multiple workloads within an organization, instead of each team using its own checklist.

5. Comparison

Well-Architected reviews workload design; Trusted Advisor provides checks and recommendations for supported accounts and resources.

AWS Trusted Advisor

1. Concept

Trusted Advisor checks an AWS environment and recommends improvements through supported check categories.

2. Purpose

AWS Trusted Advisor audits accounts at a high level and provides recommendations to improve costs, security, performance, fault tolerance, service quotas, and operations.

3. How it works

Account assessment

  • Trusted Advisor: Checks resource configuration and state to recommend improvements in an AWS account; no EC2 agent is required.
  • Examples: A public snapshot, root access keys, a nearly exhausted quota, or resources that could cost less.
  • Your action: Review recommendations and choose changes appropriate for the application.
  • Comparison: It advises; it does not block every incorrect configuration at resource creation like an enforced guardrail.
AWS account
↓
Trusted Advisor checks
↓
Recommendations
↓
Review / remediate

Check categories

  • Common main categories: Cost Optimization · Performance · Security · Fault Tolerance · Service Quotas · Operational Excellence
CategoryArchitectural example
SecurityCheck for risky public or credential configurations.
Fault ToleranceRemind the design to be more fault tolerant, for example backup or Multi-AZ are suitable.
Service QuotasWarning about quota almost running out before scaling gets stuck.
Cost OptimizationFind idle resources or more economical options.

Support plan and API

  • In the console, the level of checks available depends on the support plan.
  • Full check set and permission to use Trusted Advisor/Support API require appropriate support plan such as Business Support+, Enterprise Support or Unified Operations.
  • Programmatic access is useful when you want to get recommendations for multiple accounts or integrate with internal dashboards. Trusted Advisor API
Trusted Advisor API
↓
List checks / recommendations
↓
Aggregate by account
↓
Remediation workflow

Access and security

  • IAM controls who can see the Trusted Advisor and call the API.
  • With multiple accounts, administrators often aggregate data through AWS Organizations and appropriate cross-account permissions.
  • Trusted Advisor recommendations may contain resource configuration information, so view permissions should only be granted to those who need to operate them.
  • Console/API access using TLS; True remediation still requires permissions on the target service.

4. Practical example

Operations reviews unused resources and configuration risks, validating each recommendation before applying changes.

  • Account review periodically to detect S3/EBS/RDS public exposure, quotas are about to be full or resources are costing money.
  • Security team gathers Trusted Advisor findings from multiple accounts to prioritize remediation.
  • Before scaling a workload, check service quotas and fault-tolerance recommendations to avoid errors when increasing load.

5. Comparison

Trusted Advisor provides recommendations; Config evaluates configuration rules; Compute Optimizer focuses on usage-based rightsizing.

Summary

  • AWS Well-Architected: Questions and principles for reviewing designs against six pillars. The Tool records risks and tracks improvements.
  • AWS Trusted Advisor: Checks AWS environments and recommends improvements in cost, security, performance, and other supported areas. Available checks depend on account entitlements.

Governance, Cost & Other Services

AWS CloudFormation

1. Concept

CloudFormation uses templates to create and update AWS resources consistently instead of configuring each resource manually.

2. Purpose

Write AWS infrastructure into code to create/update resources automatically, instead of manually configuring on Console.

3. How it works

YAML / JSON Template
↓
CloudFormation
↓
Stack
↓
AWS Resources

Templates and Stack

  • Template = YAML/JSON file describing the infrastructure to create.

For example:

  • VPC · EC2 · ALB · RDS
  • Stack = real resources created by CloudFormation from templates.
  • The same template can be used for: Dev · Test · Prod

Common parts:

  • Resources = resource to create

  • Parameters = input

  • Outputs = output, for example endpoint

  • Conditions = condition whether to create a resource or not

Change Set and Rollback

  • Change set: Previews which resources CloudFormation will add, modify, delete, or replace before a stack update.
  • Rollback: If an update fails, CloudFormation attempts to restore the stack's previous state; this is not a data backup.
  • DeletionPolicy Retain: Keeps the configured resource when deleting the stack.
  • DeletionPolicy Snapshot: Takes a snapshot before deletion if the resource type supports it.

StackSets

= deploy the same CloudFormation template to:

  • Multiple AWS Accounts · + · Multiple Regions
  • Suitable for AWS Organizations / multi-account.

Drift Detection

  • Drift = The real resource is edited differently from the template.

For example:

Templates:
SG permit 443

Someone edit Console:
SG allow 22

→ Drift
  • Drift Detection help detect that difference.

Infrastructure Composer and Service Role

  • Infrastructure Composer = intuitive interface for designing CloudFormation templates.

Service Role = IAM Role that CloudFormation uses to:

  • Create · Update · Delete
  • AWS resources.

4. Practical example

A team uses one template for test and production environments and reviews change sets before updates.

5. Comparison

A template describes infrastructure; a stack is a deployment; StackSets deploy stacks across accounts or Regions.

Amazon SES & Amazon Pinpoint

1. Concept

SES sends and receives application email; Pinpoint is a legacy customer-engagement service whose support has ended.

2. Purpose

Send application transactions and notifications by email without operating a mail server.

3. How it works

  • Amazon Pinpoint reached end of support on October 30, 2026.
  • Its content below describes legacy functionality; related messaging channels continue under AWS End User Messaging.
  • SES remains a separate email service.
  • SES = send/receive email for application
  • Pinpoint = in old documents, used to divide customer groups and run campaigns

SES = Email service Pinpoint = Customer segment + Campaign

Amazon SES

  • SES = Simple Email Service

Used to send email applications such as:

  • registration confirmation
  • reset password
  • invoice
  • newsletter

Flow:

Application / Lambda
↓
SES API / SMTP
↓
Email
↓
User
  • SES helps eliminate the need to operate the mail server yourself.

SES, not Gmail/Outlook, has an inbox interface for users.

Verified Identity and Sandbox

If you want to send email using SES, you must verify:

  • email address
  • or domains
  • Sandbox = initial limit mode.

In the sandbox, you can usually only send to:

  • Verified email/domain
  • mailbox simulator

When available Production Access:

  • No need to verify recipient; still have to verify the sender

Sender always needs Verified Identity.

Amazon Pinpoint

In old documentation: Pinpoint = used to:

  • divide customer groups (segments)
  • create campaign (campaigns)
  • create customer journeys
  • engagement tracking

For example:

The customer has not yet made a purchase
↓
Segment
↓
Discount campaign
↓
Email/SMS/Push

Different:

SES = send email Pinpoint = select customer group + run campaign

  • Pinpoints in this document are mainly important to remember to distinguish roles from SES.

4. Practical example

A website sends registration confirmations through SES using a verified sender domain.

5. Comparison

SES provides application email; Pinpoint historically managed segments and campaigns rather than user inboxes.

AWS Systems Manager

1. Concept

Systems Manager remotely manages and automates operations on nodes with the required agent, permissions, and connectivity.

2. Purpose

Remotely administer EC2 and external AWS servers without having to manually SSH each machine.

3. How it works

Systems Manager
↓
SSM Agent + IAM + Network
↓
Managed Nodes
↓
Session / Command / Patch / Automation

Conditions for machine management

Machine needs:

  • SSM Agent

  • IAM permission is appropriate

  • Connect to Systems Manager

  • EC2 private subnet is still usable, you can pass VPC Endpoint.

No need to public IP or open port 22 to use Systems Manager only.

Session Manager

  • Session Manager: Opens a command-line session to managed EC2 instances or servers from the AWS console/CLI.
  • Benefits: No SSH keys, bastion host, or inbound port 22 on the instance are required.
  • Requirements: The instance needs SSM Agent, IAM permissions, and connectivity to the required Systems Manager endpoints.
  • Example: An administrator inspects logs on a private EC2 instance without exposing it publicly.
Admin
↓
Session Manager
↓
Private EC2

Run Command

  • Run Command: Runs commands remotely on one or more Systems Manager managed nodes.
  • Example: Send one command to restart a service on 100 EC2 instances instead of logging in to each.
  • Typical tasks: Run scripts, restart services, or install software.
100 EC2
↓
Run Command
↓
restart service

Patch Manager

  • Patch Manager: Checks which operating system patches are missing and installs eligible patches.
  • Patch baseline: Rules defining which patches are approved for installation.
  • Example: Scan EC2 instances, identify missing security patches, and install them in the selected maintenance window.
EC2
↓ Scan
Patch Manager
↓
Detect missing patch
↓ Install
Install patches

Maintenance Windows

  • Maintenance window: A scheduled period in which maintenance tasks may run.
  • Example: Install patches or restart services only on Sunday from 02:00 to 04:00.
  • Comparison: Patch Manager handles patches; a maintenance window schedules when tasks run.
Sunday
02:00 - 04:00
↓
Patch / Restart / Run Command

Automation

  • Automation Runbook is a multi-step automation script AWS Systems Manager Automation execute in a specified sequence/flow to manage AWS resources.
  • Instead of the admin manually manipulating each step, the runbook runs the entire process itself;
  • can call AWS API, run script, wait or request approval.

For example:

Create AMI
↓
Stop EC2
↓
Update Config
↓
Start EC2
↓
Verify

Automation = automating a multi-step process.

State Manager

  • State Manager: Maintains the desired configuration through associations running on a schedule or supported events.
  • Example: Require CloudWatch Agent on EC2; State Manager applies the configuration to install the agent when needed.
  • Memory aid: Define the desired state, then use State Manager to check and apply that configuration on the configured schedule.
Desired:
CloudWatch Agent must be installed

Actual:
EC2 does not have an Agent yet

↓
State Manager

→ return the device to the desired state

Operating conditions

  • Automation's Execution role must have permissions for each target service; Not every step requires SSM Agent on the device.
  • Session Manager can logs to S3/CloudWatch Logs when configured, but SSH tunnel/port forwarding content is not logged this way.
  • Run Command supports concurrency limits/error thresholds; Patch Manager is not used to upgrade major OS versions.

4. Practical example

Operations accesses private EC2 instances with Session Manager and patches them during a maintenance window.

Runbook creates AMI, updates configuration, and verifies EC2; Maintenance Window selects running hours; The State Manager keeps the CloudWatch Agent installed.

5. Comparison

Session Manager provides access sessions; Run Command runs commands; Automation executes runbooks; State Manager maintains configuration.

AWS Cost Explorer, Budgets & Cost Anomaly Detection

1. Concept

Cost tools show spending, set budgets, and detect unusual increases in charges.

2. Purpose

Track expenses, set budgets, and detect unusual spending.

3. How it works

  • Cost Explorer = Where was the money spent?
  • Budgets = Is the set limit exceeded?
  • Cost Anomaly Detection = Are the costs unusual?

Cost Explorer

  • Cost Explorer = view and analyze AWS costs.

You can filter/group by:

  • Service

  • Account

  • Region

  • Tags

For example:

Bill increased
↓
Cost Explorer
↓
EC2 increase?
Data Transfer increased?
S3 increased?
  • Yes Forecast to predict future costs based on previous data.

Cost Explorer = cost analysis and trends.

Savings Plans Recommendations

Cost Explorer can analyze usage and:

  • Suggested Savings Plans; to reduce costs if the workload is stable.

Yes:

  • Coverage = how much usage is covered by Savings Plan

  • Utilization = how much of the committed portion is used

This is just recommendation, do not purchase the Savings Plan yourself.

AWS Budgets

  • Budgets = set cost/usage thresholds and alert when they are near or exceeded.

For example:

Monthly Budget = $500

Forecast > $500
↓
Send Alert

You can alert according to:

  • Actual cost

  • Forecast cost

Note:

Budgets don't automatically block costs just because you create them.

  • Configurable Budget Actions to perform some action when a threshold is reached.

Cost Anomaly Detection

  • Cost Anomaly Detection = use machine learning to detect unusual cost increases.

For example:

Normal Data Transfer = $20/day

Today = $150/day
↓
Anomaly Detection
↓
Alert

Other Budget:

  • Budget = exceeds the threshold I set? Anomaly Detection = unusually different from the normal pattern?

Abnormalities can be detected even when the total cost has not exceeded the budget.

AWS Compute Optimizer

  • Compute Optimizer: Uses configuration and utilization data to recommend better-sized resources, such as a smaller EC2 instance when capacity is wasted.
  • Requirements: Opt in and provide enough monitoring history. EC2 memory data requires an agent or another supported source.
  • Before changing: Review peak demand, memory, networking, and storage; 10% average CPU alone does not identify the right smaller instance.
  • Applying changes: These are primarily recommendations; the service does not resize EC2/EBS automatically by default.
EC2 m5.2xlarge is rarely used
↓ CPU / memory if available / network / IOPS / throughput
Compute Optimizer
↓ Illustrative recommendation: m5.large if it meets the workload
Review peak load and application requirements
↓
Change configuration to reduce costs
ResourceRecommended
EC2Instance type/size is appropriate
Auto Scaling GroupRightsizing compute by ASG
EBSVolume type, IOPS, throughput
LambdaMemory is suitable
ECS FargateCPU and memory
RDS / AuroraConfigure the database appropriately
DynamoDB, NAT Gateway, ElastiCacheOptimized according to the capabilities and conditions of each supported resource

Cost Management Ecosystem, Budgets Actions, Savings Plans & Reserved Capacity

Billing & Cost ToolsSpecialized roles
Cost and Usage Report (CUR) / AWS Data ExportsExport the most detailed cost & usage reports (by hour/resource/tag) to S3 for analysis Athena or QuickSight
Cost Allocation Tags & Cost CategoriesTags label cost allocation by project/department; Cost Categories group costs according to a set of logical rules
AWS Pricing CalculatorEstimate architectural costs before deployment
AWS Billing ConductorCreate custom price lists (proforma billing, plus separate markup/discounts) for service Chargeback / Showback internally without changing the original AWS invoice collected at the Management Account
Cost Optimization HubCentralize, de-duplicate and prioritize all savings proposals (idle, rightsizing, RI/SP) across multiple Accounts/Regions
Instance Scheduler on AWSThe available CloudFormation solution automatically turns on/off EC2 and RDS according to the tagging schedule (for example, only running Dev/Test environment office hours)
  • AWS Budgets Actions: Goes beyond standard email alerts — when a budget threshold is reached, it will automatically (or wait for approval) be executed Apply IAM Policy, Apply SCP, or Stop EC2 / RDS Instances specify.
4 Types of AWS Savings PlansFlexible rangeTerm
Compute Savings PlansMost flexible: self-applied throughout EC2, AWS Fargate, and AWS Lambda (Can change Region, instance family, OS, tenancy)1 or 3 years
EC2 Instance Savings PlansDeeper drop on EC2 but fixed one Instance Family + Region (can still change AZ, size, OS)1 or 3 years
SageMaker AI Savings PlansApplies to eligible SageMaker AI compute components1 or 3 years
Database Savings PlansHourly commitments for qualified DB services (Aurora Serverless v2, next generation RDS/Aurora support, DynamoDB, ElastiCache, OpenSearch, DMS…)1 year
  • Reserved Instances / Reserved Capacity (1 or 3 years): Apply separately for each service such as EC2 RI, RDS/Aurora Reserved DB Instances, DynamoDB Reserved Capacity (RCU/WCU), ElastiCache/MemoryDB/Redshift Reserved Nodes and Redshift Serverless Reservations (RPU). In Organizations, Consolidated Billing shares Discount offers RI/SP between member accounts but does not share Zonal RI capacity reservation rights under this billing mechanism.

4. Practical example

Anomaly detection flags a bill spike; the team uses Cost Explorer to identify the responsible service.

If the bill increases, use Cost Explorer to find the service/account causing the increase; If you want to warn about budget overruns, use Budgets; If you want to rightsize EC2/EBS according to usage, see Compute Optimizer and check your workload before applying.

5. Comparison

Cost Explorer analyzes spending; Budgets tracks configured thresholds; anomaly detection identifies unexpected spending patterns.

Compare tools

ServiceFunction
CloudWatchCollect metrics/logs, monitor performance and alarms
Compute OptimizerAnalyze metrics/configuration and recommend resource optimization
Cost ExplorerAnalyze costs/usage and trends
BudgetsActual/forecast alerts according to set thresholds
Cost Anomaly DetectionDetect costs that are unusual compared to the pattern
Data Lifecycle ManagerAutomatically manage scheduled EBS snapshots / EBS-backed AMIs
  • Billing data has latency.
  • Forecasts are forecasts, not guaranteed prices; See upcoming needs before purchasing Savings Plans.
  • Budget Actions need their own IAM role/permissions; Permission to view invoices does not automatically grant permission to stop EC2.
AWS Outposts

1. Concept

Outposts places AWS-managed infrastructure at your site and connects it to an AWS Region.

2. Purpose

Bringing AWS infrastructure to the business's datacenter to run workloads on-site but still using the familiar management method of AWS.

3. How it works

AWS Region
↕ Service Link
AWS Outposts at Datacenter
↓
Local Workloads

What is AWS Outposts?

  • Outposts = AWS hardware is set right at your datacenter.
  • Can run some supported AWS resources on-prem.

Suitable when needed:

  • low latency with local system

  • Process/save data at specific location

  • still want to use AWS tools/API

Not all AWS services can run on Outposts.

Connect to AWS Region

Outposts still connect back AWS Region cha via:

  • Service Link
Outposts
↓ Service Link
AWS Region
  • Service Link is used for traffic management and connection to AWS Region.

Outposts is not a standalone AWS cloud that is completely offline.

Responsibility

  • AWS = Outposts infrastructure management.

Enterprise = prepare:

  • datacenter

  • electricity

  • cooling

  • network

  • application/data

  • IAM, Security Group and encryption still need normal configuration.

4. Practical example

A factory evaluates Outposts for processing near equipment while retaining AWS operating models.

5. Comparison

Outposts brings compute/storage on premises; Direct Connect provides connectivity; Storage Gateway provides hybrid storage access.

Outposts vs Direct Connect vs Storage Gateway

ServiceWhat to use?
OutpostsRun AWS compute/resources right at the datacenter
Direct ConnectPrivate network line from on-prem to AWS
Storage GatewayConnect on-prem storage to AWS storage

Remember:

Outposts
= AWS runs at On-Prem

Direct Connect
= On-Prem connection ↔ AWS

Storage Gateway
= connect Storage On-Prem ↔ AWS
AWS Batch

1. Concept

AWS Batch schedules and provides compute for batch jobs, such as processing many image files.

2. Purpose

Run large batch jobs without having to manage the server/scheduler yourself.

3. How it works

Job Definition
↓
Submit Job
↓
Job Queue
↓
Scheduler
↓
Compute Environment
↓
Container Job

Main ingredients

Job Definition = configure how the job will run:

  • Container image
  • Command
  • CPU / RAM
  • IAM Role
  • Job = a particular run.
  • Job Queue = queue contains jobs that have not yet run.
  • Compute Environment = resources used to run the job, usually:
  • EC2
  • Fargate
  • EC2 Spot if the job can withstand interruption

For example:

1000 images need to be processed
↓
Submit 1000 Jobs
↓
Job Queue
↓
AWS Batch provides compute on its own
↓
Containers handled
↓
Output → S3

AWS Batch = Queue + Scheduler + Compute for batch jobs.

IAM

  • Job Role = permissions of the code inside the container.

For example:

Batch Job
↓ Job Role
S3 Read / Write
  • Permission to submit Batch job does not mean the container itself has access to S3.

4. Practical example

Thousands of video files enter a job queue; Batch allocates compute and runs jobs according to resource requirements.

5. Comparison

Batch manages job queues and scheduling; Fargate provides container compute; Lambda runs event-driven functions with its own limits.

Batch vs Lambda vs Fargate

  • AWS Batch = coordinate multiple batch jobs, suitable for heavy/long-running jobs.
  • Lambda = event-driven function, suitable for short tasks; Maximum normal lambda 15 minutes.
  • Fargate = where containers run without server management; can do compute under AWS Batch.

Batch = job management / queue / scheduling Fargate = provides compute for the container to run

Multi-Node Parallel (MNP) & Batch Scheduling Policy vs EventBridge Scheduler

Mechanism on AWS BatchFunctions & Limitations to remember
Multi-Node Parallel (MNP)Allows a large HPC/ML job to run simultaneously on multiple EC2 nodes coordinating via MPI (main node coordinates child nodes); Not supported on Fargate
AWS Batch Scheduling PolicyFair-share and prioritize resources among job groups in the Job Queue (no cron timer)
Amazon EventBridge SchedulerDecision time Trigger a job to run periodically (e.g. every day at 8:00 a.m. send the job to AWS Batch or ECS)
Amazon AppFlow

1. Concept

AppFlow transfers data between SaaS applications such as Salesforce and supported AWS services.

2. Purpose

Transfer data between SaaS applications and AWS services without writing a lot of integration code yourself.

3. How it works

SaaS / AWS Source
↓
AppFlow
↓ filter / map / transform
Destination

For example:

Salesforce
↓
AppFlow
↓
S3 / Redshift

Source and Destination

  • AppFlow uses connector to connect source/destination.

Possible:

  • map fields

  • filter data

  • validation

  • transform is simple

  • Not all connectors support all transmission directions.

AppFlow = managed data flow between SaaS ↔ AWS.

When does Flow run?

  • On Demand = runs when the user calls
  • Schedule = run periodically
  • Event = runs when source has change event and connector support

For example:

Salesforce update
↓
AppFlow
↓
S3

Scheduled flows can:

  • Full = get all data

  • Incremental = only get new/changed data

Security

AppFlow supports:

  • encryption in transit

  • encryption at rest

  • IAM

  • KMS

  • SaaS credentials / OAuth

  • If destination is S3/Redshift, appropriate permissions are still needed.
  • PrivateLink only applies when the connector supports it.

4. Practical example

Salesforce data is transferred to S3 on a schedule for analytics.

5. Comparison

AppFlow serves SaaS integration; Glue performs ETL/data integration; DMS focuses on database data migration.

AppFlow vs Glue vs DMS

  • AppFlow = Simple SaaS integration
  • Glue = ETL / transform data is more complicated
  • DMS = migrate/replicate database

For example:

Salesforce
↓ AppFlow
S3
↓ Glue
Transform Data

Amazon EventBridge Scheduler

1. Concept

EventBridge Scheduler invokes services once at a specified time or on a recurring schedule.

2. Purpose

Run tasks according to time/schedule without needing to maintain the cron server yourself.

3. How it works

Rate / Cron / One-time
↓
EventBridge Scheduler
↓
Execution Role
↓
Target

Schedule types

  • Rate = repeats after a period of time.

For example:

rate(5 minutes)

  • Cron = run on a specific schedule.

For example:

Every day 08:00

  • One-time = runs exactly once at the set time.

Rate = every time, Cron = any time according to the schedule, One-time = runs once.

Target and Execution Role

Scheduler can call:

  • Lambda
  • SQS
  • Step Functions
  • many other AWS APIs

Flow:

It's time
↓
Scheduler assumes Execution Role
↓
Call Target

Execution Role Must have appropriate permissions such as:

  • invoke Lambda
  • send message SQS
  • start Step Functions

Retry and DLQ

  • Retry: Calls the target again after a failed attempt.
  • DLQ: A queue that holds undelivered events for investigation and later processing.
  • Avoid duplicates: The target may be called again; use a request ID to avoid repeating an effect, such as sending the same invoice twice.
Scheduler
↓
Retry
↓ still fails
SQS DLQ

4. Practical example

A schedule invokes Lambda nightly to generate reports, with retries and a DLQ for target failures.

5. Comparison

Scheduler triggers by time; an EventBridge event bus reacts to events; Batch scheduling chooses job execution order.

Scheduler vs EventBridge Event Bus

Scheduler = trigger according time

For example:

Every night 01:00
↓
Run Lambda

EventBridge Event Bus = trigger according event

For example:

EC2 state changed
↓
EventBridge Rule
↓
Lambda
AWS AppConfig

1. Concept

AppConfig deploys configuration and feature flags with validation, gradual rollout, and rollback.

2. Purpose

Implement secure application configuration changes and feature flags with validation and automatic rollback without the need to build/deploy code again.

3. How it works

Protection layers during configuration rollout

ComponentProtection function
ValidatorsCheck syntax/semantics using JSON Schema or Lambda function before Enables configuration release
Deployment Strategy & Bake TimeRelease slowly at a rate (Linear, Canary) and then keep the follow-up period (Bake Time) after reaching 100%
CloudWatch Alarm + Auto RollbackAutomatically stops and returns to the previous stable configuration as soon as CloudWatch Alarm reports an increase in error rate/latency

4. Practical example

A team releases a feature to a subset of users and rolls configuration back when its CloudWatch alarm fires.

5. Comparison

Parameter Store stores values; AppConfig controls configuration deployment; Secrets Manager focuses on secrets and rotation.

Distinguish SSM Parameter Store vs AWS AppConfig

CriteriaSSM Parameter StoreAWS AppConfig
Main purposeStores configuration key-value pairs and secret strings (SecureString)Dynamic configuration distribution & feature flags Controlled operational risks
Validation, Gradual Rollout & Auto RollbackThere is no built-in canary/auto-rollback mechanism according to CloudWatch AlarmFull support schema/Lambda validation, canary/linear rollout, and automatic rollback
AWS Service Catalog, License Manager & AWS Managed Services (AMS)

1. Concept

Service Catalog provides approved infrastructure products, License Manager tracks licenses, and AMS operates AWS environments.

2. Purpose

Standardize the self-provisioning of approved infrastructure, software licensing control, and outsourcing the operation of multi-account enterprise environments.

3. How it works

AWS Service Catalog & AWS License Manager

Administration serviceMechanism of action & Distinguishing point
AWS Service CatalogManage approved infrastructure portfolio (Product written in CloudFormation compiled in Portfolio attach IAM permissions and launch constraints) for groups to create themselves Provisioned Product compliant with security standards/tags without needing permission to create raw resources or write your own IaC
AWS License ManagerCentralized management of commercial licenses (Oracle, Windows/SQL Server, SAP...) on both AWS and On-premises via License Configuration count and block exceeding the limit vCPU, Cores, Sockets or Instances (integrates EC2 Launch, Dedicated Hosts, Organizations)
Platform Admin defines CloudFormation Template → puts it into Service Catalog Portfolio → Developer selects allowed Products → creates standard-compliant AWS Resources

AWS Managed Services (AMS)

AMS Model/ComponentOperating range
AMS AccelerateAdd 24/7 monitoring, incident detection, patching and backup on existing environments; The customer team still directly changes the infrastructure
AMS Advanced & MALZOperate strictly on Multi-Account Landing Zone (MALZ); Infrastructure changes go through the process RFC (Request for Change) or approved self-service

4. Practical example

Employees provision environments from approved Service Catalog products with controlled permissions and configurations.

5. Comparison

  • Service Catalog governs approved provisioning;
  • License Manager handles licenses;
  • AMS supports operations within its service scope.

Summary

  • AWS CloudFormation: Describes infrastructure in templates for consistent provisioning and updates. A stack is the deployed resource group; a change set previews updates.
  • Amazon SES & Pinpoint: SES sends application emails such as confirmations and invoices. Pinpoint previously supported customer campaigns but has reached end of support.
  • AWS Systems Manager: Manages fleets centrally. Session Manager provides remote access, Run Command executes commands, and Patch Manager handles patching.
  • AWS Cost Tools: Cost Explorer shows where money was spent. Budgets alerts on configured thresholds. Cost Anomaly Detection identifies unusual spending.
  • AWS Outposts: Places AWS infrastructure in your data center for local processing, with service-specific connectivity and dependencies on an AWS Region.
  • AWS Batch: Queues jobs and provisions compute to run them. Suits workloads such as rendering and file processing without building a scheduler.
  • Amazon AppFlow: Transfers data between SaaS applications and AWS using configured flows, such as Salesforce data into S3.
  • AWS Amplify: Helps build and deploy web/mobile applications. Hosting can connect to Git to build and deploy frontend updates.
  • EventBridge Scheduler: Invokes services on recurring schedules or once at a specific time. A role authorizes target calls; retries and a DLQ handle delivery failures.
  • AWS AppConfig: Changes configuration and feature flags without redeploying code. Supports validation, gradual rollout, and rollback on configured failure conditions.
  • Service Catalog, License Manager & AMS: Service Catalog provisions approved infrastructure. License Manager manages software licenses. AMS supports AWS operations within its service scope.
